A gate whose easiest exit is weakening itself will eventually be weakened; that this instance was escalated instead is a fact about the operator, not about the mechanism." proposed_solution="give charters an explicit scope (a slice-id field, or a per-slice subdirectory) and have the examine gate select ONLY charters matching the entering slice, degrading LOUD when a feature-scope charter is the only one present -- naming the missing slice charter and routing to `des charter-scaffold` to ADD it, never inviting an edit of the feature-scope one; the feature-scope charter stays the feature-end oracle"
- [ ] po-agent-cannot-run-its-own-producing-tool: paradigm=object-oriented defect="nw-product-owner is the natural owner of expectation-charter authoring, but its toolset is Read/Write/Edit/Glob/Grep/Task with NO Bash, so it structurally cannot run `des charter-scaffold` (the producing tool) nor `des verify-charter-filled` (its own gate). Dispatched to author a charter via those commands it correctly refused to fabricate their output and hand-wrote the file to the documented dialect instead, leaving the gate run to the dispatcher. That is the honest failure, but it means the GDP-5 'system produces the artifact' property is unavailable to the role that owns it, and charter authoring silently degrades to hand-assembly plus a hope that the dialect was matched by eye. Note the shape: an instruction that points at a capability the addressee does not have -- the same class as a dispatch advisory naming a command its own envelope cannot invoke." proposed_solution="either grant nw-product-owner a scoped Bash capability sufficient to run the des charter commands, or expose charter-scaffold/verify-charter-filled through a tool the agent already has -- so the authoring role can run its own producing tool and its own gate"
- [ ] examine-verdict-is-not-bound-to-the-content-it-attests: paradigm=object-oriented defect="The examine ledger records a verdict with a timestamp and NOTHING binds that verdict to the CONTENT it was rendered against. A recorded PASS therefore survives arbitrary later edits to the very code the examiner exercised -- it is a rubber stamp with a date on it. Observed live 2026-07-22 in worktree collect-error-indeterminate: ledger shows FAIL at 10:16:40 then PASS at 11:19:50 (the correct shape -- examined, failed, fixed, re-examined, passed), while the production files carry mtimes of 12:26:32 and 12:33:27, roughly 70 minutes AFTER the verdict. Whether the CONTENT changed is a separate question that mtime cannot answer, and that is exactly the point: neither the operator nor the gate can currently tell, because the only freshness signal available is a filesystem timestamp that moves for reasons unrelated to content (checkout, formatter, identical re-save). THE PATTERN ALREADY EXISTS IN THIS CODEBASE AND IS SIMPLY MISSING HERE: the RED seal IS bound to its test file's content and is VOIDED when the file changes -- `des verify-red-green --record-red` re-runs are required after any edit for precisely this reason. So the project already knows the correct design, applies it to RED, and does not apply it to EXAMINE. Sibling asymmetry again, the fourth instance found today: two mechanisms doing the same KIND of thing (attesting to a state of the code) where one is content-bound and the other is not, with nothing holding them to a shared standard. AGGRAVATING: the verdict is the LAST gate before commit, so a stale PASS converts directly into a false attestation on the commit record -- the failure lands in the audit trail, which is the one place it must not." proposed_solution="store a hash of the examined artifacts alongside the verdict (the same way RedObserved binds to its test file), and have `des commit-slice` recompute it and REFUSE with what/why/how when it differs -- naming which artifact changed and routing to a re-examine, so staleness is mechanical rather than a matter of somebody noticing that two clocks disagree. Emit the same check as an explicit ExamineVerdictStale outcome distinct from ExamineVerdictMissing, since 'never examined' and 'examined against different code' need different remediation. Do NOT use mtime for this -- it is not a content signal."
- [ ] examiner-unscoped-collection-prohibited-in-writing-and-done-anyway-twice: paradigm=object-oriented defect="A prohibition that has now failed TWICE IN WRITING is not going to succeed on the third telling, and that is the finding -- not the individual disobedience. MEASURED 2026-07-22, two consecutive `nw-user-examiner` dispatches: examiner #1 ran a whole-tree `pytest --co -q` in the TRUNK checkout despite a dispatch naming only two commands (observed independently from outside her worktree, in a process survey: PID 95143 `/home/alexd/Projects/nWave-dev/.venv/bin/python3 -m pytest --co -q`, contributing to the box load being diagnosed at that moment). The dispatcher then tightened the wording exactly as asked -- 'FORBIDDEN, not merely unrequested', plus 'if you are about to invoke pytest without naming a specific file or directory, STOP'. Examiner #2 ran `uv run pytest tests/des/acceptance/ --co -q` anyway. Two for two against escalating prose. WHY IT COSTS: an unscoped collection on this repo walks 6800+ files, is one of the most fork-hungry things available on a shared box, and lands during exactly the window when a gate elsewhere is being judged -- so it manufactures the starvation that makes OTHER lanes' reds indeterminate. It also silently widens an examiner's exposure to the codebase she is supposed to be blind to. THE GENERAL SHAPE, and it is GDP-2 read in reverse: enforcement belongs at the mechanism, guidance belongs inline at the authoring surface, and PROSE IN A DISPATCH PROMPT IS NEITHER -- it is a request that a probabilistic agent is free to not honour, dressed as a control. Escalating the wording is the move that feels like progress and measurably is not." proposed_solution="stop rewriting the prohibition and BUILD the mechanism: wrap the examiner's test invocation in a runner that REFUSES an unscoped collection (no file or directory argument -> exit non-zero with what/why/how naming the scoped form), or add a PreToolUse hook that blocks a bare `pytest`/`pytest --co` from an examiner-role dispatch. Keep the prose as the inline affordance (GDP-2) so the block is rarely reached, but the prose must stop being the only thing standing between an examiner and a whole-tree walk. Pair with the related finding that SELF-REPORT of a blindness violation is necessary but NOT sufficient -- an examiner under-reported her own violations (confessed one `grep -r`, the transcript audit found a second, a `cat` of a marker file under tests/) -- so the transcript audit must be a PERMANENT step, not a temporary measure while the clause is new."
- [ ] mainline-first-parent-history-carries-no-attestation-at-all: paradigm=object-oriented defect="MEASURED 2026-07-22: of the 12 `fix`/`feat` commits on trunk's FIRST-PARENT mainline in the last 12 hours, ZERO carry a `Slice-Id:` trailer, zero carry `Gate-Scope:`, and most carry no `Co-Authored-By:` either. The attestation is not destroyed -- each attested commit IS an ancestor of trunk, verified -- but it lives on the merged-in SECOND parent, while the commit that sits on the mainline is a merge whose SUBJECT LINE DUPLICATES the attested fix verbatim. So `git log --oneline` shows every fix TWICE, and the copy on the mainline (the one a reader naturally inspects, the one `--first-parent` returns) is completely bare. Four pairs confirmed with identical file and line counts: f61bd0e1b/ff337d851, 8a69e6b24/da64aba54, 3219919e3/9e8d40035, ce8df9c0f/d0aa4c9a6. HONEST SCOPE, because I checked rather than assumed: I did NOT establish that any gate breaks because of this. The `--first-parent` traversal that exists in `git_subprocess.py:170-189` is used for ANCESTRY/reachability, not for trailer discovery, and the trailer readers (`attest_bundled_slice.py`, `domain/slice_id_trailer.py`) do not appear to walk first-parent. So this is filed as an AUDIT-TRAIL defect -- the mainline history reads as entirely unattested to a human or to any tool that walks it the obvious way -- and NOT as a broken gate. Whether some verifier does depend on it is unverified and is the first thing to check before designing a fix. WHY IT STILL MATTERS AT THIS SCOPE: the whole point of the trailers is that the audit trail is mechanically checkable after the fact, and the branch the trailers live on is deletable -- once a lane's worktree and branch are cleaned up, whether the second parent stays reachable is a garbage-collection question, not a guarantee." proposed_solution="FIRST establish whether any verifier walks first-parent for trailers -- if one does, this is urgent rather than cosmetic. Then either propagate the attestation onto the merge commit (a merge-time hook that lifts `Slice-Id:`/`Gate-Scope:` from the branch tip into the merge message), or make merge-back fast-forward/rebase so the attested commit IS the mainline commit and no duplicate subject exists. Do not solve it by teaching every reader to check second parents: the audit trail's value is that it is obvious, and an audit trail you need a technique to read is one nobody reads."
- [ ] examine-dispatch-does-not-name-which-surface-renders-the-charter-detail: paradigm=object-oriented defect="An examine dispatch names the charter and the product but does not consistently name WHICH shipped surface renders the detail that charter's oracle needs -- and it matters most exactly where it is least obvious: when the oracle is per-item or structural rather than a simple pass/fail. Reported by sister alpha-challenge 2026-07-22: ~10 minutes lost to blind CLI-versus-dashboard trial and error on one dispatch, because the same product exposes the same fact through several surfaces and only one of them renders it at the granularity the oracle asks for. The reporter framed it as their own dispatch discipline rather than a tool bug; it is BOTH, and the skill-level half is the one that generalises -- every orchestrator rediscovers it separately, and the cost is paid once per dispatcher instead of once per framework. It also compounds the sibling defect where an examiner routes around a surface she cannot make sense of: an examiner who is not told WHICH surface has more room to improvise into the internals." proposed_solution="make the surface a REQUIRED field of the examine dispatch template -- the concrete URL, CLI invocation, or screen that renders the oracle's detail -- and have `des dispatch` refuse to generate an examine envelope without it, per GDP-5 (the system produces the artifact, the operator does not hand-assemble it). Where the charter's oracle is per-item or structural, require the dispatcher to name the surface that renders it AT THAT GRANULARITY, since the coarse surface passing is not evidence for a per-item claim."
- [ ] charter-provenance-is-unrecorded-so-contamination-is-unfalsifiable: paradigm=object-oriented defect="The expectation-charter disqualification rule -- an author holding the design or implementation CANNOT produce an uncontaminated charter, which is why the bugfix flow authors it at t=0 before the fix exists -- is a discipline with NO mechanical trace whatsoever. Nothing records WHO authored a charter, in WHAT context, or at what point relative to the code it judges: the template carries no provenance field, no charter telemetry exists under `.nwave/telemetry/`, and no ledger records an authoring event. So a downstream reader cannot establish whether any given charter was authored clean, and the rule is unfalsifiable in exactly the direction that matters. OBSERVED 2026-07-22 in worktree collect-error-indeterminate: for `fix-na-marker-reconcile-drift` the file mtimes run test 11:52:25 -> implementation 12:05:40 -> CHARTER 12:13:26, all within one dead agent's session. The charter was written LAST, after both the test and the implementation existed. Its TEXT is clean -- no leaked function or module names -- and that is precisely the trap: textual cleanliness is not uncontamination, because the rule is about the author HOLDING THE DESIGN, not about vocabulary leaking onto the page. A charter written after the fact can steer its oracle toward scenarios the implementation already handles, in perfectly abstract language. WHAT MTIME CAN AND CANNOT ESTABLISH, stated because it is the whole difficulty: it establishes the file WRITE ORDER; it does NOT establish that the same context authored it, since a fresh PO dispatched afterwards would also write last. So contamination here is SUSPECTED and not PROVEN, and there is no artifact anywhere that could settle it. That is the defect. Compare the sibling mechanism that gets this right: `verify-red-green --record-red` binds the RED seal to the test file's CONTENT and voids it on edit. Charters -- which are the OTHER half of the same attestation chain -- carry no binding at all, to content or to author. Sixth sibling asymmetry of the day. MEASURED EVIDENCE OF WHAT CONTAMINATION ACTUALLY COSTS, 2026-07-22 -- a controlled comparison, same bug, two authors, one holding the implementation. The post-hoc charter for `fix-na-marker-reconcile-drift` (written AFTER its test and implementation) framed its negative oracle around "does the block explain itself" and split the epistemic-gap symptom TWO ways: "you have work left" vs "I cannot tell". A fresh author given ONLY the observable split the SAME symptom THREE ways -- "I could not check this" vs "checked, it is fine" vs "checked, it is not fine" -- naming a silent-false-fine AND a silent-false-broken as two DISTINCT failure modes. The fresh charter also demanded the full 2x2 matrix (tracking off/on x complete/incomplete) hold in all four cells, where the contaminated one asserted the coverage-ON half only abstractly. So contamination narrowed coverage in EXACTLY the predicted direction: the post-hoc author, anchored to the fix already built around a hardcoded 3-entry list, checked what that fix already covered and was vaguer about what it did not. The charter text was textually clean throughout -- no leaked names -- which is the point: contamination shows up as NARROWED SCOPE, not as leaked vocabulary, so a grep-for-implementation-nouns check cannot detect it. Note also that the fresh author independently reached the epistemic-vs-ontological three-way distinction, making it the THIRD clean-room author tonight to derive tonight's dominant defect class from the observable alone." proposed_solution="record charter provenance as a first-class artifact at authoring time: the authoring agent id, the dispatch id, and a hash of the inputs it was given (the observable statement) -- emitted by the producing tool `des charter-scaffold` so the operator never hand-asserts it (GDP-5). Then a downstream gate can REFUSE, or at minimum degrade LOUD, when a charter has no provenance record or when its provenance shows it was authored by a context that had already seen the implementation. Until that exists, adopt the cost-asymmetric default and write it into the skill: when charter provenance CANNOT be established, treat the charter as CONTAMINATED and re-author from the observable -- a fresh PO dispatch is cheap and cloud-bound, while a green earned against a contaminated oracle means nothing and is indistinguishable in the ledger from a real one."
- [ ] walking-skeleton-gate-refuses-an-operator-authored-file-with-no-how: paradigm=object-oriented defect="`walking_skeleton_gate.py` refuses on a file the OPERATOR authors by hand -- `walking-skeleton.json` -- and every one of its refusals gives WHAT without HOW: `unparseable walking-skeleton.json: {exc}` (:119), `walking-skeleton.json is not a JSON object` (:121), `walking-skeleton.json missing string 'feature_root'` (:171), `walking-skeleton.json missing list 'entry_points'` (:234). MEASURED: the file contains ZERO occurrences of `schema`, `scaffold`, `doctor` or `template` -- so a maintainer told their manifest is missing `entry_points` is given no schema to consult, no example to copy, and no command to generate it. They are left to reverse-engineer the expected shape from the gate's own source, which is exactly the investigation a self-explaining refusal exists to make unnecessary. THIS IS A GDP-4 VIOLATION SPECIFICALLY, not merely a terse message: the project already ships a family of producing tools for precisely this class of artifact -- `charter_scaffold.py`, `feature_delta_doctor.py`, `feature_end_preconditions_scaffold.py`, `flavor_scaffold.py` -- so the pattern of 'the system generates the checked artifact, the operator never hand-assembles it' is established, implemented four times over, and simply not applied here. Either no scaffold exists for this manifest (then the gate is the signal to build one, GDP-4's own remedy) or one exists and the refusal does not name it (then the fix is one line). SCOPE, stated honestly because the raw count overstates it: `src/des/cli/` + `src/des/application/` carry 44 raise/exit sites against only 24 `Fix:` markers, but MANY of those raises are INTERNAL invariant violations (e.g. `prompt_rendering_service.py`: 'Command cannot be None or empty') where the audience is a programmer and the remediation is 'fix the caller' -- applying the operator-facing what/why/how mandate to those would be over-applying it. The defect is the OPERATOR-FACING subset: a gate reading a hand-authored file and refusing it without naming how to produce a correct one. Confirmed operator-facing instances beyond the manifest: `verify_environmental_e2e.py:277` 'build produced no wheel under {dir}' and `:357` '--fixture-junit-xml resolved to no paths'. Counter-example proving the codebase knows better: `roadmap.py:30` refuses with the EXPECTED FORM inline -- `Invalid steps spec '{pair}', expected 'PHASE_ID:COUNT'`." proposed_solution="triage the 44 raise/exit sites into OPERATOR-FACING and INTERNAL-INVARIANT, and hold only the first class to what/why/how -- a blanket sweep would add noise to programmer-facing assertions where it does not belong. For each operator-facing refusal, route the HOW to a PRODUCING TOOL per GDP-4: add `des walking-skeleton-scaffold` (mirroring the four scaffolds already shipped) and have every manifest refusal name it, so the remedy is a command to run rather than a shape to guess. Where no tool exists yet, the gate is the signal to build one. Add a test asserting that operator-facing refusals contain a remediation marker, so the class cannot silently return -- and pick the marker over prose so the assertion is mechanical."
- [ ] background-agent-completion-notifications-are-dropped-silently: paradigm=object-oriented defect="Background sub-agent completion notifications go missing, and the loss is silent -- the orchestrator simply waits forever for a signal that will never arrive. OBSERVED 2026-07-22 by the drain-silent-noop lane: TWICE in one session (an RCA dispatch, then a crafter dispatch) the agent finished its work and no completion notification reached the dispatching orchestrator. It only recovered because it had, after the FIRST loss, armed its own backstop watcher on transcript idleness; without that improvised guard it would have blocked indefinitely on the second. WHY IT IS EXPENSIVE OUT OF PROPORTION TO ITS FREQUENCY: the whole parallel-lane design depends on dispatch-and-be-notified. A dropped notification does not present as an error -- it presents as an agent that is still working. So the orchestrator's correct behaviour (wait rather than poll, do not spam a running agent) is exactly what converts a lost message into an unbounded stall, and the failure is indistinguishable from slowness until someone gets suspicious. Same family as the rest of tonight's pile: an ABSENCE OF SIGNAL read as a state of the world (still running) rather than as an unknown. RELATED HAZARD reported in the same message, and it is a genuine race rather than a defect in itself: messages sent to a running agent are QUEUED and delivered at its NEXT tool round, so a RETRACTION of an instruction races the agent acting on the original. Tonight a wrong instruction was retracted in time by luck, not by design -- the agent happened not to have reached its next tool call. NOT VERIFIED by me directly; reported by the lane with its own timeline. Filed because it is the second independent instance and the recovery mechanism was improvised rather than provided." proposed_solution="give the orchestrator a POSITIVE liveness signal rather than relying on a terminal one -- a heartbeat, or a queryable agent state that distinguishes running / finished / died, so 'no notification' can be resolved into one of those rather than defaulting to 'still running'. Failing that, make the backstop first-class instead of leaving each lane to improvise a transcript-idleness watcher: an orchestrator-side timeout that escalates INDETERMINATE ('no completion signal in N minutes; the agent may have finished, stalled, or died') per GDP-6 degrade-LOUD. For the retraction race, expose message-delivery status to the sender so a time-critical correction can be known to have landed BEFORE the agent acts, rather than hoped to have."
- [ ] negative-at-seal-verifies-presence-not-scope: paradigm=object-oriented defect="`des verify-negative-at` passes on presence and naming convention (a `@pytest.mark.negative_at` marker or a `_not_` substring in a test name), never on the SPACE the negatives actually cover. CONFIRMED 2026-07-22 on a controlled pair: a charter-contaminated draft test authored post-hoc around a hardcoded 3-entry list PASSED with 2 negative ATs found, while a clean-room charter for the same bug demanded a strictly larger space (three-way distinguishability of 'could not check' from either verdict, plus a full 2x2 matrix in all four cells) that the draft never approaches. So the mechanical seal certifies a suite that checks only what its own implementation already handles. This is the SAME blind spot as the charter check one layer down: contamination manifests as NARROWED SCOPE, not as leaked vocabulary or missing markers, so neither a grep for implementation nouns nor a count of negative markers can detect it. Both seals answer 'is something there' when the question is 'is enough there'." proposed_solution="derive the required negative space from the CHARTER's oracles rather than from marker presence: map each charter negative bullet to at least one covering test and REFUSE (or degrade LOUD) when a bullet has no mapped test, naming which bullet is uncovered. Presence-of-marker stays as a cheap floor but must stop being the whole check. Until that exists, the fresh-charter-then-diff procedure is the only detector we have and its cost is one cloud PO dispatch."
- [ ] wave-floor-should-advise-not-hard-stop-between-waves: paradigm=object-oriented defect="The inter-wave floor (`.nwave/wave-active/active.json`, armed with `provenance: inferred` by the PreToolUse fallback in `pre_tool_use_handler.py`) HARD-STOPS sub-dispatches with `WAVE_MARKER_BYPASS` (exit 2, decision:block) whenever an in-wave child drops the DES markers -- e.g. a DESIGN sub-dispatch, or a read-only finder, denied because a stale/inferred `deliver` or `distill` floor is armed. MEASURED 2026-07-22..23: repeatedly blocked unrelated dispatches (read-only techdebt finders, a nw-solution-architect DESIGN dispatch), each requiring a manual `des wave-clear`. Ale 2026-07-23: the between-waves block has become an IMPEDIMENT and should be an ADVISORY, not a hard stop -- a suggestion to the user to read the docs DISCUSS produced (first) then DESIGN produced (after), not a denial. The hard stop is GDP-1-miscalibrated: it fires reactively at dispatch time on an INFERRED (not human-declared) floor, blocking legitimate work, and its only escapes are claim-false-membership or clear-the-floor (already filed: inferred-wave-floor-blocks-non-wave-dispatch-with-only-dishonest-escapes). Converting hard-stop→advisory removes the impediment while keeping the guidance." proposed_solution="downgrade the inferred-floor bypass from decision:block(exit 2) to an ADVISORY warning printed to stderr/context that POINTS the operator at the wave outputs to read in order (DISCUSS feature-delta / user stories, then DESIGN architecture + component boundaries) and proceeds. Keep a HARD block ONLY for a floor a human or a wave command actually declared (provenance != inferred). Route via the DES spine (production change to `wave_activation_service` / `pre_tool_use_handler` / `des_marker_parser`); add a test that an inferred floor warns-and-allows while a declared floor still blocks. Blast radius: measure via des blast-radius before lane selection."
- [ ] examiner-observations-not-handed-to-dispatching-suborch: paradigm=object-oriented defect="A dispatched nw-user-examiner returns its PASS/FAIL verdict + concrete observations in its completion notification (delivered to the top-level dispatcher), but the SUB-ORCHESTRATOR that dispatched it does not receive that report automatically — it must explicitly resume the examiner to read the observations text. record-examine-verdict --observations requires the examiner's real observation words (a disciplined sub-orch refuses to fabricate them), so the seal stalls until the report is manually retrieved. MEASURED 2026-07-23 on TWO lanes (lean-suite fix-gate-collect-spawn-plugin-overhead + validator fix-validator-refuses-own-generated-dispatch): both examiners finished PASS, both left .nwave/telemetry/examine/ empty, both sub-orchs sat ahead=0 waiting; unblocked only when the top-level orchestrator relayed the observations from the completion notification it alone had received." proposed_solution="Either (a) have the examine dispatch write the examiner's full verdict+observations to a known file/ledger path the dispatching sub-orch reads (not only the completion notification), or (b) document in the sub-orch dispatch pattern that after an examiner completes it must read the agent's completion output/transcript for the observations rather than waiting for a pushed message. Do NOT make the examiner self-seal record-examine-verdict — separation of examine from verdict-recording is intentional (prevents self-rubber-stamping)."
- [ ] examine-slice-key-feature-end-vs-commit-slice-slice-nn-mismatch: paradigm=object-oriented defect="`des dispatch --phase FEATURE_END_EXAMINE` auto-corrects --slice to the literal 'feature-end', so record-examine-verdict seals the verdict keyed 'feature-end'; but `des commit-slice` hard-requires a slice-NN identity and looks up the examine verdict under slice-NN, so it does not find the 'feature-end'-keyed verdict and the operator must MANUALLY re-record the same PASS under --slice slice-01 before commit-slice accepts it. MEASURED 2026-07-23 on collect-error lane, BOTH bugs (na-marker + primary-collect): same underlying PASS re-recorded twice purely to re-key the slice identity. Pure ceremony — the two subsystems disagree on the slice key for one examine." proposed_solution="Reconcile the slice-key contract between the FEATURE_END_EXAMINE dispatch/record path and commit-slice's lookup: either have commit-slice accept a 'feature-end'-keyed examine verdict for a slice-NN commit, or stop the dispatch auto-correcting --slice to 'feature-end' when a slice-NN was given. Add a test that a FEATURE_END_EXAMINE PASS recorded once is accepted by commit-slice without a manual re-record."
- [ ] drain-batch-merge-blocked-deletes-unmerged-work-asymmetry: paradigm=object-oriented defect="src/des/application/refactor_drain_service.py: on a FAILED (blocked) merge, drain_one (single-item, ~line 226-229) deliberately does NOT clean up — it preserves the unmerged branch+worktree for human recovery, enforced by the passing AT test_an_unmerged_branch_is_never_deleted_after_a_failed_merge (test_slice_01_merge_and_cleanup.py:121-144, docstring cites a spike incident where unconditional cleanup deleted an unmerged branch). BUT _drain_concurrently (batch, ~line 320-322) does the OPPOSITE: its merge-blocked branch runs remove_worktree+delete_branch inline, silently discarding the unmerged work the single-item path is contractually required to preserve. Batch and single-item modes disagree on merge-blocked semantics. Found during the orphan-cleanup RCA 2026-07-23." proposed_solution="Reconcile the two: batch merge-blocked should preserve the unmerged branch+worktree exactly as single-item does (do NOT delete on failed merge). Extend test_an_unmerged_branch_is_never_deleted_after_a_failed_merge to parametrize over both drain_one and drain_batch so the contract is enforced for both paths. Note: this is behavior-changing for batch, so route via /nw-bugfix with its own AT + Vera examine, not folded into the orphan-cleanup fix."
- [ ] commit-slice-path-stages-whole-file-cross-contaminates-shared-file: paradigm=object-oriented defect="`des commit-slice --path <file>` stages the ENTIRE file, not only the entering slice's hunks. When two logically-separate fixes coexist in one worktree and share a production file (e.g. two bugs both editing src/des/application/feature_end_cycle_service.py), committing the first with --path <shared-file> sweeps the SECOND fix's already-written hunks into the first fix's commit under the first fix's message — a mixed commit whose Gate-Scope/SliceCommitVerified then attests content that does not belong to that slice. MEASURED 2026-07-23 on collect-error lane: BUG A's commit-slice swept 100% of BUG B's FullSuiteLegRan changes (excluded_directories/exclusion_reason + _pruned_top_level_dirs helper) into BUG A's commit; caught only by manually diffing the committed hunks, fixed via git reset --soft HEAD~1 + unstage-the-shared-file + scoped recommit. Whole-file path granularity cannot separate interleaved changes." proposed_solution="commit-slice should build its commit from a temporary index seeded with HEAD plus only the slice's OWNED HUNKS (hunk-level), or at minimum detect that a --path file carries hunks outside the slice's declared change-set and REFUSE with a what/why/how (name the foreign hunks, point to `git add -p` / reverse-apply). Mirror the `des commit --owned-paths` correct-by-construction temp-index approach but at hunk granularity for the shared-file case. Add a test: two fixes sharing one file, commit-slice of the first must not include the second's hunks."
- [ ] freshness-gate-blocks-vera-real-cli-examine-in-dev-checkout: paradigm=object-oriented defect="The runtime freshness gate (which blocks executing a stale installed `des` in a developer checkout, detected via .git adjacency) ALSO blocks a surface-pinned Vera EXAMINE from invoking the real `des refactor` CLI when the worktree is a developer checkout. MEASURED 2026-07-23 (fix-drain-orphan-cleanup EXAMINE): Vera could not run the real CLI end-to-end — the freshness gate refused — so she fell back to the in-process 85-test suite. Verdict was still conclusive (PASS) but the examiner's core value (real-surface probing, which has caught defects every mechanical layer missed) was degraded to test-suite reading. The safety gate (prevent stale-runtime execution) and the examiner (must probe the REAL surface) are in tension: the gate fires on the examine context it should exempt." proposed_solution="Exempt a surface-pinned examine invocation from the freshness gate (e.g. an explicit NWAVE_EXAMINE_SURFACE=1 / --examine-surface flag that asserts the caller has pinned `uv run --project <worktree>` so the runtime is the editable source under test, not a stale installed copy), OR have the examine-fixture producing tool build a non-dev-checkout drivable repo so the real CLI runs without tripping the gate. Add a test that a surface-pinned examine can execute the real des CLI in a dev-checkout worktree."
- [ ] wave-guard-inspects-dispatch-prompt-content-and-self-blocks-suborch: paradigm=object-oriented defect="The WAVE_MARKER_BYPASS guard on an Agent/Task dispatch inspects the DISPATCH PROMPT TEXT for wave-context keywords (des dispatch / EXAMINE / commit-slice / feature-end / deliver / DES markers) and, if it detects partial wave-context WITHOUT a full DES-VALIDATION marker, classifies the dispatch as a markerless wave-owned child and DENIES it (exit 2) — even when the dispatch is an independent sub-orchestrator prompt that merely DESCRIBES the spine workflow it will drive. MEASURED 2026-07-23: an identical sub-orchestrator lane was denied 5 consecutive times with elaborate prompts mentioning those concepts, then dispatched successfully on the 6th attempt with a LEAN prompt that omitted the trigger keywords — with NO persistent background des process arming the floor between attempts (the floor read 'clear' immediately before each denied dispatch and 'clear' immediately after), isolating the arm to the dispatch's own PreToolUse hook path scanning the prompt. Combined with the inferred-provenance hard-stop, this makes resuming a parked lane a coin-flip on prompt wording." proposed_solution="The wave-guard must key membership off an explicit, structured marker (DES-WAVE / DES-VALIDATION) ONLY — never off free-text keyword scanning of the prompt body, which false-positives on any prompt that merely documents the spine vocabulary. A sub-orchestrator dispatch that carries no DES markers is NOT a wave-child and must pass regardless of what workflow concepts its prose mentions. Add a test: a dispatch prompt containing the strings 'des dispatch', 'EXAMINE', 'commit-slice', 'feature-end' but no DES-VALIDATION/DES-WAVE marker is NOT treated as a wave-bypass. Interim workaround: keep sub-orch dispatch prompts lean, free of wave keywords."
- [ ] examine-pass-does-not-attest-which-oracle-rows-were-exercised: paradigm=object-oriented defect="A recorded EXAMINE verdict (record-examine-verdict) captures PASS/FAIL + free-text observations but does NOT attest WHICH charter oracle rows the examiner actually exercised. An examiner that probes only a SUBSET of a feature-scoped charter's oracle (e.g. only the spawn-behavior rows, never the drain-report row) can still record a full PASS, and commit-slice then gates green on that latest-PASS — a false-green. MEASURED 2026-07-23 (fix-inherited-stdin-deadlocks-spawns): a late-completing examine PASSed on spawn/stdin/timeout/reaping behavior only, never exercising the #1 _report()/merge_blocked_reason path that the same feature-scoped charter requires (still unfixed); its ledger then carried FAIL followed by PASS for the same feature_id + charter_seal, and only a human diff of observations-vs-oracle caught that the PASS was scope-incomplete. commit-slice trusting the latest PASS would have shipped an unmet charter." proposed_solution="record-examine-verdict should require the examiner to enumerate which oracle rows it exercised (a checklist keyed to the charter's oracle), and mark the verdict INCOMPLETE (not PASS) if any oracle row was not exercised. commit-slice must reject a verdict that does not attest full oracle coverage for the charter's scope. Alternatively, bind the verdict to a charter-oracle-row hash so a PASS that omits rows is mechanically detectable. Add a test: an examine PASS that exercised only N-1 of N oracle rows is not accepted by commit-slice."
- [ ] feature-end-success-report-opaque-no-leg-names-no-test-count: paradigm=object-oriented defect="the FeatureEndCycleComplete payload reports only numeric leg COUNTS (leg_census ran/not_applicable/indeterminate) - it never names WHICH legs ran or were skipped, and it discards the executed-test count it already holds (the full-suite junit at .nwave/telemetry/feature-end/<feature>-suite.junit.xml records tests=48 on a fixture where the visible report says only ran:2). A user therefore cannot see from the report that their suite actually ran, nor which leg was skipped and why - the same produce-then-discard anti-pattern as the collect-error bug this was found alongside. Found by Vera examining charter a-passing-verdict-means-my-tests-actually-ran (2026-07-22); 3 flags, all on the SUCCESS path, none a regression from the collect-error fix" proposed_solution="surface the full-suite executed-test count into the success payload and name each leg in the census (additive keys keep the 9 test files asserting on the existing leg_census keys green); needs its own ATs, not a bolt-on"
- [ ] bugfix-lane-can-start-implementation-before-any-charter-exists: paradigm=object-oriented defect="on bugfix/collect-error-is-indeterminate-not-absent, after landing and committing its charter-governed slice (fix-collect-error-indeterminate, Vera PASS), the same lane started a SECOND bug (primary collect shadowed by a generated dir) implementation-first: a 260-line regression test plus a production diff to feature_end_cycle_service.py accumulated on disk with NO docs/product/expectations/ charter anywhere for it, and no des dispatch/RCA artifact naming it either. The agent died mid-flight and left it in that state. Nothing in the tooling refused this -- des dispatch, verify-red-green and the test-authoring path all accept a regression test with no charter behind it; the ONLY place the omission would have surfaced is commit-slice's examine gate, arms-on-charter, which the lane never reached. This is not a case of an agent skipping a known step -- it is that between one slice's COMMIT and the next slice's first write, nothing checks or reminds that Phase 0-charter (t=0, before any code) is owed again. MEASURED 2026-07-22: reconstructed from the dead agent's recovered working tree, not from any log naming the gap." proposed_solution="give the bugfix/atdd_pure per-slice entry a proactive-inline affordance (GDP-2) at the FIRST write after a prior slice's COMMIT: if a new tests/bugs/test_bug_*.py or equivalent regression-test path is about to be authored and no docs/product/expectations/fix-{slug}/ directory exists yet for that slug, warn before the write lands, naming the missing charter and routing to Phase 0-charter (fresh nw-product-owner, observable-only) as the HOW (GDP-4). Reactive backstop: des verify-red-green (or an equivalent early-RED check) could also refuse to record RedObserved for a test file whose feature-id has no charter directory, degrading loud rather than silently letting 260 lines accumulate uncatchable until commit-slice."
- [ ] dispatched-agents-inherit-orchestrator-session-cwd-and-misroute-to-wrong-worktree: paradigm=object-oriented defect="When the session-orchestrator's own cwd is one of the worktrees it dispatches into (here /home/alexd/Projects/wt/spawn-stdin-deadlock, the environment primary dir), EVERY dispatched sub-agent inherits that cwd and physically lands there instead of the worktree it was assigned — so relative-path reads/writes and identity inference (`git status` in the inherited cwd) resolve to the WRONG worktree. MEASURED 2026-07-23, bit TWICE: (1) a sub-orch assigned to feature A found itself in the session cwd, saw feature B's staged work, and began working B (caught only when it re-read the charter and noticed the mismatch); (2) a fresh bugfix lane spawned into the wrong worktree, mis-identified as a different lane, and correctly REFUSED to execute instructions for a worktree not its own. Damage: agents working/dirtying the wrong worktree, cross-contaminated .nwave ledgers/task-markers. Distinct from the DES-PROJECT-ROOT marker gap (that's des dispatch not emitting the marker; this is the harness cwd inheritance itself)." proposed_solution="Every dispatch prompt must force, as the agent's FIRST action: cd <assigned-worktree-absolute>, verify with `git -C <abs> rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD` matches the expected branch, ABORT if not; use absolute paths everywhere (relative resolves to the inherited cwd); inject the project-root marker; and after each sub-dispatch verify the other worktrees stayed untouched. Better: the dispatch producing-tool should bake the cd+verify preamble in by construction. Also instruct agents to REFUSE instructions for a worktree != their own (the discipline that prevented wrong-worktree writes tonight). Root harness fix: dispatched agents should default their cwd to the assigned worktree, not inherit the orchestrator's."
- [ ] examine-surface-pin-instruction-insufficient-needs-mechanical-binary-verification: paradigm=object-oriented defect="Instructing the examiner to drive `uv run --project <worktree> des ...` is NOT enough to guarantee it tests the code under test: the examiner can still (drop --project, run from a cwd uv doesn't resolve to the worktree project, or invoke bare des) fall back to the PATH and resolve to the GLOBAL INSTALLED shim (~/.claude-alt/bin/des -> ~/.claude/lib/python/des), a STALE pre-fix snapshot -> false-FAIL on code that is actually correct. MEASURED TWICE 2026-07-23 (drain-silent-noop, then spawn-stdin slice-02 signal-handler): both false-FAILed because the examiner probed the stale installed runtime; diagnosis proved the worktree code was correct (readlink -f showed the examiner hit ~/.claude/lib/python/.../refactor.py, 9449 bytes pre-fix, vs 25333 bytes in the worktree). The surface-pin as a prose instruction is not mechanically enforced." proposed_solution="The examine dispatch/producing-tool must MECHANICALLY verify, as a hard preamble the examiner runs before any probe, that `readlink -f \"$(uv run --project <worktree> which des)\"` resolves INTO the worktree (<worktree>/.venv/... or the editable src), and ABORT with INDETERMINATE(surface-unverified) if it resolves to the installed shim. Better: the examine-fixture producing tool emits the exact verified invocation + the readlink assertion by construction, so the examiner cannot silently test the wrong binary. Relates to the already-filed examine-surface-staleness + freshness-gate rows — this is the enforcement-not-instruction escalation."
- [ ] drain-real-ctrl-c-sigint-pgroup-still-orphans-subtree-uv-wrapper-race: paradigm=object-oriented defect="The slice-02 signal-cleanup fix reaps correctly on SIGTERM-to-the-top-PID (verified PASS) but NOT on a real operator Ctrl-C (SIGINT to the foreground process GROUP). MEASURED 2026-07-23 by a binary-verified nw-user-examiner (readlink confirmed the worktree's des, not the stale shim): `kill -INT -<pgid>` -> exit 130, empty message, grandchild sleep 600 alive, 5 orphan procs (uv, des, sh, stub, sleep), leftover branch+worktree refactor-TD-4b. Violates the charter negative 'no leftover process once my prompt is back (normally, early, or after Ctrl-C)'. Likely root: SIGINT to the pgroup hits both the uv wrapper AND des main simultaneously; uv dies via default disposition and kills its child des main before `_abort_on_signal`'s reap completes -- whereas SIGTERM-to-the-single-top-PID lets uv forward to des which handles it cleanly. Deeper tension: `start_new_session=True` on the agent subtree (needed so the timeout-reap's killpg targets only that child's group) also means a pgroup Ctrl-C never reaches the subtree directly, so ALL reaping depends on des main's handler surviving long enough -- which it does not under pgroup-SIGINT-with-a-dying-uv-parent. A prior diagnosis claimed pgroup-SIGINT reaped; the demanding examiner refutes it. NOTE: unresolved whether the INSTALLED des shim invocation (no uv wrapper) behaves differently from `uv run des` on Ctrl-C -- needs checking." proposed_solution="DESIGN-territory (process-supervision architecture), not a trivial patch: reconcile the timeout-reap's need for a separate child session with the Ctrl-C-reap's need to reach the subtree. Options to weigh in design: (a) des main installs the SIGINT handler to reap FAST + re-raise, and the invocation avoids the uv-wrapper death race (e.g. the shim exec-replaces so there is no wrapper parent in prod; verify the installed-shim Ctrl-C path); (b) a lightweight supervisor/pidfile that reaps even if des main is killed mid-handler; (c) block/mask the signal in des main until the reap completes. Route through /nw-design -- flagged for human decision, not forced. Interim: the SIGTERM path works; verify-worktree-cleanup remains the backstop for leftover git state."
- [ ] repo-root-resolution-lacks-single-composition-root-prefactoring-not-marker-patch: paradigm=object-oriented defect="ROOT of the cwd-contamination class (reclassifies the DES-PROJECT-ROOT / dispatched-agents-inherit-session-cwd rows). nWave HAS a canonical resolver (src/des/domain/repo_path_resolver.py:resolve_repo_root: --repo-root -> NWAVE_REPO_ROOT env -> Path.cwd() fallback) but centralization is PARTIAL: (a) it FALLS BACK to Path.cwd() when no flag/env is passed -- which is exactly where a dispatched agent, inheriting the orchestrator session's cwd, resolves to the wrong repo; (b) ~74 files touch repo-root and some resolve Path.cwd() AD-HOC, bypassing the resolver entirely (e.g. housekeeping_service.py:56 `Path.cwd() / '.nwave'`, log_persistence.py:105). So writer and reader of the same logical root resolve from DIFFERENT sources -> silent disagreement (the cwd-contamination that derailed lanes all night). Cross-diagnosed 2026-07-23 with Lyra-evolution: same common root as her backup-dir bug (LYRA_DATA_DIR unified only the read-view; write paths from independent env-vars) -- NO single composition root for a logical resource -> scattered N-site resolution -> silent consumer disagreement. A 'unify by setting one env/param' only covers a subset of sites." proposed_solution="This is a @PREFACTORING, not a bugfix of the gate (per the standing rule: scattered-resolution fix needs the resolver extracted BEFORE the behavioral fix -- patching a single site hits the trap). Shape: (1) route ALL repo-root/.nwave resolution through resolve_repo_root (eliminate the ad-hoc Path.cwd() sites); (2) have `des dispatch` set NWAVE_REPO_ROOT=<target-worktree> in the generated envelope so a dispatched agent's resolver picks the worktree, not the inherited-cwd fallback; (3) thread the resolved root as an injected capability, never re-resolve ad-hoc in consumers. Route through /nw-design (prefactoring) -- the DES-PROJECT-ROOT marker injection is only an interim mitigation, not the fix."
- [ ] consolidation-signal-tick-cannot-record-observed-healthy-trunk: paradigm=object-oriented defect="`des consolidation-signal-tick` NON puo' registrare un segnale trunk-health OSSERVATO-verde, mentre il loop di consolidamento (Loop 3/9 never-idle) istruisce l'orchestratore a `Record an OBSERVED trunk-health signal ... via des consolidation-signal-tick` a ogni fire. MISURATO 2026-07-24: la whitelist di `signal_type` nel dominio accetta SOLO segnali negativi -- `['drift', 'failing-gate', 'stale-branch', 'unmerged-work']` -- e rifiuta qualunque cosa altro con `CONSOLIDATION_SIGNAL_INTAKE_REJECTED: unsupported signal_type 'trunk-health'`. Non esiste un signal_type per 'trunk pulito / nessun segnale negativo osservato', quindi l'istruzione del loop e' letteralmente insoddisfacibile sul percorso felice: quando il trunk E' sano (il caso piu' frequente in steady-state) non c'e' NULLA da registrare e ogni fire diventa un no-op forzato. Contraddizione loop<->contratto-tool. Friction secondaria stesso comando: `--signal` e' un prefisso AMBIGUO fra `--signal-type` e `--signal-key` (argparse abbreviation), quindi un'invocazione naturale `--signal trunk-health` esce 2 con `ambiguous option`. Distinta dalla riga 101 (quella copre il SILENZIO sul success-path di un intake ACCETTATO; questa e' l'IMPOSSIBILITA' di accettare un intake positivo)." proposed_solution="Due opzioni. (A) Se il tick e' progettato SOLO per segnali negativi (una coda di consolidamento raccoglie problemi, non salute), allora il loop e' scritto male: Loop 3 non dovrebbe chiedere di registrare la SALUTE ma solo di registrare un segnale NEGATIVO quando osservato, altrimenti no-op onesto -- e il messaggio di rifiuto dovrebbe dirlo ('trunk-health non e' un segnale di consolidamento; registra solo drift/failing-gate/stale-branch/unmerged-work quando OSSERVATI, altrimenti nessuna azione e' corretta'). (B) Se si vuole davvero un heartbeat positivo, aggiungere un signal_type `healthy`/`trunk-green` che il dominio accetti e che serva da battito osservato. In ogni caso: allineare la prosa del loop e la whitelist del tool -- oggi si contraddicono, e l'operatore che segue il loop alla lettera colpisce un rifiuto a ogni fire su trunk pulito. Minore ma gratis: dare a `--signal-type`/`--signal-key` alias non-ambigui o rifiutare `--signal` con un hint esplicito."
- [ ] blast-radius-counts-consumers-by-bare-symbol-name-not-qualified-module: paradigm=object-oriented defect="`des blast-radius` conta i consumatori per NOME-SIMBOLO NUDO invece che per simbolo QUALIFICATO-DAL-MODULO, quindi ogni funzione che condivide un nome comune eredita il conteggio aggregato di TUTTE le omonime del repo. PROVA MISURATA 2026-07-24 (due sonde su moduli non-correlati): `des blast-radius --repo . --paths src/des/cli/consolidation_signal_tick.py` riporta `consumer_counts['consolidation_signal_tick.main'] = 333`; `--paths src/des/cli/dispatch.py` riporta `consumer_counts['dispatch.main'] = 333`. Numero IDENTICO su due moduli che non si chiamano fra loro -- non e' una coincidenza, e' il conteggio aggregato dei 213 `def main(` presenti in src/ + scripts/. Controprova sullo stesso comando: un simbolo con nome DISTINTIVO si comporta bene (`lane_profile.LaneProfile` = 4, plausibile), quindi il difetto morde selettivamente i nomi comuni (main, run, setup, __init__...) e NON e' visibile finche' non si misura un simbolo generico. CONSEGUENZA sul routing, che e' il punto: `large_min_consumers=10`, quindi QUALUNQUE modulo con una funzione `main()` viene classificato tier=L per costruzione -- tutti gli 87 CLI sotto src/des/cli/ inclusi. Il classificatore che deve decidere la CORSIA (S diretta / M one-shot / L a slice) manda in L-lane lavoro che e' S o M, cioe' impone DISCUSS/DESIGN a fix meccanici. Misurato in vivo: la riga defects 'consolidation-signal-tick-success-path' -- aggiungere UNA riga di output sul percorso di successo -- e' stata classificata tier=L con reason `consumer_counts[main]=333 >= 10`. E' il difetto peggiore possibile per un gate di misura: non e' silent-wrong sul permissivo (non lascia passare l'L come S), e' rumoroso sul restrittivo, quindi non si manifesta mai come incidente -- si manifesta come cerimonia che sembra dovuta, e nessuno la mette in discussione perche' il gate 'ha misurato'. AGGRAVANTE MISURATA 2026-07-24, e cambia la classe del difetto: il verdetto sbagliato NON resta nella cerimonia, ENTRA NEI RECORD DI VERITA'. Committando il bugfix `validate-manifest-missing-file-msg` (1 file di produzione, +27 righe, nessun boundary, consumatori reali <=1 -- tier S per qualunque lettura onesta) `des commit-slice --tier S` ha RIFIUTATO con `BlastRadiusTierExceeded`, reason `consumer_counts[validate_component_manifest.main]=333` E `consumer_counts[test_validator_missing_file_message.CliResult]=29` -- due conteggi per nome nudo, il secondo su un helper di test con un nome comune, che mostra che il difetto non tocca solo `main` ma qualunque identificatore condiviso. Le uniche uscite offerte erano dichiarare `--tier L` o spezzare la slice; ho dichiarato L, e ORA IL COMMIT PORTA IN PERMANENZA UNA CLASSIFICAZIONE FALSA prodotta dal difetto stesso. Un difetto di MISURA si propaga cosi' nel RECORD che quella misura doveva certificare -- il trailer del commit, il ledger, e a valle ogni analisi di tier-distribution che li legga. Non e' piu' 'un gate rumoroso': e' una sorgente di dati falsi nella storia del repo, e i dati falsi sopravvivono al fix del gate." proposed_solution="Qualificare il conteggio per modulo: il consumatore va risolto sul simbolo IMPORTATO-DA-QUEL-MODULO (`from des.cli.dispatch import main` / `dispatch.main(...)`), mai sul nome nudo `main` ovunque compaia. Dove l'analisi AST non riesce a risolvere l'origine di una chiamata, il conteggio deve degradare LOUD (INDETERMINATE per quel simbolo, mai un numero che sembra misurato -- GDP-6): un conteggio non-attribuibile presentato come conteggio e' esattamente il silent-wrong che il gate esiste per impedire. AT obbligatoria e gia' riproducibile: due moduli non-correlati che espongono entrambi `main` DEVONO produrre conteggi diversi, e un modulo il cui unico chiamante e' un test non deve ereditare il conteggio delle omonime. Aggiungere anche un oracolo negativo: il tier di un modulo NON deve cambiare rinominando una sua funzione da `helper_x` a `main` senza toccare nessun chiamante -- oggi cambia, e questa e' la formulazione piu' corta del difetto."
- [ ] wiring-slice-closes-feature-while-leaving-an-upstream-slice-unwired: paradigm=object-oriented defect="UNA SLICE DI WIRING CHIUDE LA FEATURE CABLANDO SOLO UN SOTTOINSIEME DELLE SLICE A MONTE, E NULLA LO RILEVA. Caso misurato 2026-07-24 su F-DESIGN-COMPONENT-MANIFEST (5 slice, tutte con test implementati, nessuna skipped): il simbolo `resolve_manifest_state` (prodotto da slice-03 'four-state-resolver') compare in slice-03 e in NESSUNA altra slice -- conteggio per-slice sui .feature: 01=0, 02=0, 03=3, 04=0, 05=0. Slice-05 si chiama 'wiring' e si dichiara 'the final slice wires the manifest into the design wave's exit review', ma cabla la VALIDAZIONE (slice-01/02, `validate_component_manifest` -- vivo, usato dal gate DESIGN in produzione) e il PRODUCER (slice-04), lasciando il RESOLVER scollegato. La prosa di slice-05 lo confessa nel descriversi -- 'Read in sequence after slice-04: slice-04 made the manifest produced; this slice makes its absence or wrongness block the design wave' -- nomina 04, nomina la validazione, e SALTA 03 senza dichiararlo. Conseguenza a valle, gia' materializzata: un detector never_wired ha segnalato il resolver come orfano, la riga di pile risultante e' stata instradata al drain, e il crafter ha CANCELLATO modulo + test + il .feature di slice-03 -- cioe' una specifica -- perche' dal singolo simbolo 'zero chiamanti di produzione' e' indistinguibile da 'codice morto'. La cancellazione e' stata fermata prima del commit solo perche' un umano ha guardato le slice SORELLE. LA CLASSE: il meccanismo di slicing traccia l'ORDINE (slice-05 dopo slice-04) ma non la COPERTURA (slice-05 cabla TUTTE le slice a monte?). Un fan-in che dichiara di raccogliere tutti i rami e ne raccoglie tre su quattro e' indistinguibile da uno completo. Stessa forma degli altri difetti di copertura-parziale-presentata-come-completa gia' filati (examine-PASS che non attesta quali oracle-row ha esercitato; report feature-end che conta le gambe invece di nominarle)." proposed_solution="Due livelli. (1) IL CASO: il difetto NON e' 'rimuovi resolve_manifest_state' -- e' 'slice-05 non cabla slice-03'. Decidere se il four-state resolver va cablato nel design-exit review accanto alla validazione, oppure se slice-03 e' stata superata dal design e va ritirata ESPLICITAMENTE (ritiro dichiarato, non cancellazione dedotta da un detector). (2) IL METODO, che vale piu' del caso: introdurre un gate di WIRING-COMPLETENESS. La slice di wiring deve ATTESTARE quali slice a monte cabla (elenco esplicito, non prosa), e il gate rifiuta se una slice a monte della stessa feature resta senza consumatore vivo dopo il wiring. Cosi' 'ramo non cablato' diventa un fatto dichiarato e verificabile invece di un'assenza silenziosa che solo un detector a valle notera' -- male, e a distanza di mesi. Corollario per la corsia di drain: una riga di pile prodotta da un detector che AVVERTE dei propri falsi positivi non deve essere drenabile in autonomia; il verdetto 'orphan/dead/unused' su un simbolo va sempre allargato alle sue SORELLE (le altre slice della stessa feature) prima di autorizzare una rimozione -- costa una `ls`, e qui avrebbe evitato la cancellazione di una specifica."
- [ ] pile-has-no-duplicate-or-composition-detection: paradigm=object-oriented defect="Il pile dei difetti non ha alcun rilevamento di RIGHE DUPLICATE ne' di COPPIE-CHE-SI-COMPONGONO, e nessuno dei due si vede leggendo le righe una per una -- che e' l'unico modo in cui le righe vengono lette. MISURATO 2026-07-24 raggruppando le 179 righe di defects.md per file citato (regex sui path src/|scripts/|nWave/|tests/, un comando): (a) DUPLICATO CONFERMATO -- `dispatch-feature-end-examine-falls-through-to-crafter` e `feature-end-examine-phase-dispatches-crafter-not-examiner` sono lo STESSO difetto, stessa causa (`src/des/cli/dispatch.py:168`, `_PHASE_AGENTS` non mappa FEATURE_END_EXAMINE) e stesso sintomo (l'envelope emette `Agent: nw-software-crafter`), filati in momenti diversi arrivandoci da due strade; (b) COMPOSIZIONE non descritta in nessuna scheda -- su `dispatch.py` + `nWave/agents/nw-user-examiner.md` convergono quattro righe (i due duplicati sopra + `examiner-blindness-claimed-by-construction-is-only-by-instruction` + `examine-discipline-lives-only-in-prose-with-no-mechanical-guard`) che insieme dicono una cosa che NESSUNA dice da sola: la prova-di-done di una feature e' affidata a un agente capace di leggere il sorgente, e la cecita' che lo impedirebbe e' garantita solo per istruzione; (c) seconda composizione -- su `scripts/build_dist.py` convergono `refactor-actuator-shipped-nowhere-drain-is-noop-on-every-consumer` e `catalog-names-actuator-by-relative-path-absent-on-consumer`, che insieme significano che la corsia di drain e' documentata e INERTE su ogni macchina che non sia quella di sviluppo. PERCHE' E' UN DIFETTO DEL MECCANISMO e non un caso singolo: le righe vengono classificate e prioritizzate INDIPENDENTEMENTE, quindi due 'minori' che condividono un meccanismo restano minori ciascuna a casa propria e nessuna viene mai promossa, benche' composte producano una modalita' di fallimento qualitativamente nuova; e un duplicato gonfia il denominatore e rende il difetto piu' facile da rimandare, perche' due righe minori pesano meno di una riga sola." proposed_solution="Un check meccanico sul PILE (non sul codice), eseguibile come loop ricorrente e non come analisi ad-hoc: raggruppare le righe per file/simbolo citato e segnalare (1) i gruppi con >=2 righe come CANDIDATI-COMPOSIZIONE da valutare insieme, (2) le righe che condividono lo stesso locus + lo stesso sintomo come CANDIDATI-DUPLICATO da fondere. Il raggruppamento e' una regex sui path piu' un defaultdict -- il costo e' un comando, e al primo giro su 179 righe ha prodotto un duplicato confermato e due composizioni. Aggiungere al momento del FILING un campo esplicito che nomini il meccanismo toccato (non solo il file), cosi' il raggruppamento non dipenda dal fatto che due autori diversi abbiano citato lo stesso path. Nota di processo: la fusione dei duplicati e la promozione delle composizioni CAMBIANO la priorita' del pile, quindi sono decisioni umane -- il check deve SEGNALARE le coppie, mai fonderle o ri-prioritizzarle da solo."
- [ ] pile-rows-are-not-freshness-checked-and-compose-into-phantom-blockers: paradigm=object-oriented defect="LE RIGHE DEL PILE NON HANNO ALCUNA VERIFICA DI FRESCHEZZA CONTRO IL CODICE, e un'analisi che le COMPONE amplifica quella staleness in un blocker che non esiste. MISURATO 2026-07-24: un composition-finder sul pile ha riportato come BLOCKER la coppia `dispatch-feature-end-examine-falls-through-to-crafter` + `validator-refuses-dispatch-its-own-generator-produces`, concludendo che 'la fase e' nella mappa del generatore ma non in quella del validator, quindi 41 feature armate per l'examine non possono essere dispacciate'. Ragionamento internamente CORRETTO e conclusione FALSA: verificato nel sorgente, `src/des/cli/dispatch.py:180` mappa gia' `ATDDPurePhase.FEATURE_END_EXAMINE` all'agente esaminatore, e `src/des/application/atdd_pure_prompt_validator.py:268-282` e' stato RIPROGETTATO per chiavare sull'AGENTE risolto invece che sulla FASE -- il suo docstring cita quel difetto AL PASSATO ('FEATURE_END_EXAMINE was refused by the very generator that produced it') come la ragione stessa del redesign. Entrambe le righe sono gia' fixate e ancora aperte `- [ ]`. LA CLASSE, che e' peggiore del singolo caso: un pile e' un registro di INTENZIONI, non uno specchio dello STATO. Una riga stale presa da sola e' solo rumore -- chi la lavora scopre subito che e' gia' fixata. Ma DUE righe stale COMPOSTE producono un blocker fantasma piu' convincente di ciascuna, perche' la composizione aggiunge un ragionamento nuovo e plausibile ('nessuna delle due blocca da sola, insieme garantiscono il fallimento') che nessuna delle due righe conteneva e che quindi nessuno riconosce come gia' risolto. Il metodo che abbiamo introdotto per trovare i rischi nascosti EREDITA e AMPLIFICA la staleness del suo input. Conseguenza secondaria sul denominatore: se due righe su una coppia campionata erano gia' chiuse, il pile a 180 righe sovrastima il lavoro residuo di una frazione ignota -- e ogni priorizzazione basata sul conteggio e' tarata su un numero gonfio." proposed_solution="Due livelli. (1) FRESHNESS-CHECK PRIMA DI COMPORRE, non dopo: il composition-finder deve, per ogni riga che entra in una composizione, RI-VERIFICARE nel sorgente che il difetto sia ancora presente, e marcare `STALE-CANDIDATE` (non `CONFIRMED`) qualunque riga il cui locus citato non mostri piu' il difetto. Una composizione che contiene anche UNA riga non ri-verificata va riportata come IPOTESI, mai come blocker. (2) FRESHNESS-SWEEP PERIODICO SUL PILE, indipendente dal finder: un passaggio read-only che per ogni riga aperta apre il file:riga citato e segnala i candidati-gia-fixati -- senza chiuderli da solo, perche' chiudere una riga e' un giudizio umano. La riga di pile dovrebbe portare, al filing, il commit-sha del momento in cui il difetto e' stato osservato, cosi' che 'quanto e' vecchia questa osservazione' sia un dato e non una congettura. Nota di metodo generale: vale per QUALUNQUE analisi derivata da un artefatto-registro (pile, backlog, catalogo, inventario) -- l'analisi eredita la freschezza dell'artefatto, mai quella del sistema, e va etichettata con la freschezza della sua FONTE."
- [ ] examine-attestation-is-more-ephemeral-than-the-commit-it-authorises: paradigm=object-oriented defect="LA PROVA SOPRAVVIVE MENO DELL'OGGETTO CHE PROVA. `.nwave/` e' GITIGNORED (verificato 2026-07-24: `git check-ignore -v .nwave/telemetry/examine/<feature>.jsonl` -> `.nwave/.gitignore:2:*`; `git ls-files .nwave/` restituisce solo `.gitkeep` e `local-config.json`, zero ledger). Quindi al termine di una corsia bugfix: il COMMIT viaggia sul trunk portando il suo `Gate-Scope:` sigillato e il `Slice-Id:`, mentre il VERDETTO EXAMINE che lo ha autorizzato -- il record `ExamineVerdictRecorded` con verdict, observations e charter_seal -- resta nel worktree e MUORE quando il worktree viene rimosso. MISURATO in vivo sulla chiusura di `validate-manifest-missing-file-msg`: il ledger e' stato scritto in `/home/alexd/Projects/wt/validator-missing-file-msg/.nwave/telemetry/examine/`, ed e' ASSENTE dal repo principale; il commit `a2f055f72` invece e' permanente. Fra una settimana, di quella catena (RED sigillato + GREEN + PASS su quattro oracle-row con output citato + charter_seal) sopravvivera' SOLO il commit. CONSEGUENZA: 'questa slice e' stata esaminata' torna a essere un'AFFERMAZIONE di chi c'era invece di un FATTO consultabile -- esattamente il punto di partenza da cui i ledger dovevano tirarci fuori, e la ragione per cui esiste il mandato earned-trust (evidenza meccanica, non verdetto asserito). SI COMPONE con due difetti gia' filati, e insieme danno TRE modi indipendenti di perdere la stessa prova, nessuno dei quali emette un segnale: (a) l'evento non viene mai scritto (`hook-diagnostic-logging-swallows-its-own-failure-without-a-trace`, techdebt); (b) viene scritto nell'albero sbagliato (`repo-root-resolution-lacks-single-composition-root`); (c) viene scritto correttamente e poi svanisce col worktree (questa riga). Tutti e tre a monte di una firma di gate terminale." proposed_solution="Il criterio generale: un artefatto di PROVA deve avere una durata MAGGIORE O UGUALE a quella dell'artefatto che certifica; se e' minore, la certificazione ha una data di scadenza che nessuno ha dichiarato. Opzioni, non esclusive: (1) al momento del commit-slice, incorporare l'ESSENZIALE dell'attestazione nel commit stesso -- il verdetto, il charter_seal e l'examiner id come trailer accanto a `Gate-Scope:` -- cosi' la prova viaggia con cio' che prova ed e' verificabile da chiunque legga la storia; (2) promuovere i ledger di attestazione (esaminate, verdetti, sigilli) da `.nwave/` gitignored a un percorso TRACCIATO, distinguendoli dalla telemetria volatile che giustamente resta ignorata -- oggi i due tipi di dato condividono lo stesso destino e hanno esigenze opposte; (3) come minimo immediato, un gate che al merge-back RIFIUTI se l'attestazione della slice non e' stata trasferita nel target, invece di lasciarla morire in silenzio nel worktree. Verificare anche il gemello: quali ALTRI record di verita' (AtCompletionLedger, verdetti di review, sigilli RED) vivono sotto `.nwave/` e condividono la stessa scadenza non dichiarata."
- [ ] claude-home-assumed-canonical-instead-of-resolved-breaks-alt-profiles: paradigm=object-oriented defect="LA HOME DI CLAUDE E' ASSUNTA IN POSIZIONE CANONICA invece che RISOLTA dall'ambiente, e questo rompe per chiunque non usi il profilo di default. SEGNALATO da un utente (Attila, 2026-07-24: 'la home di claude deve essere recuperata e non assunta come se fosse in posizione canonica per tutti'). VERIFICATO nel sorgente, due forme distinte: (a) FALLBACK HARDCODED -- `src/des/domain/agent_capability.py:175` ritorna `Path.home() / \".claude\"`; (b) PEGGIO, MATCH PER STRINGA -- `src/des/application/tdd_template_loader.py:40` decide se e' un'installazione con `\".claude\" in module_str or \".claude\" in module_resolved_str`, e `:47` risale i parent cercando `parent.name == \".claude\"`. Cioe' l'identita' della directory di installazione e' dedotta dal suo NOME. CHI SI ROMPE: chiunque imposti `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` a un percorso che non si chiama `.claude` -- e non e' un caso ipotetico, e' la NOSTRA configurazione documentata: CLAUDE.md descrive tre profili (`~/.claude`, `~/.claude-alt`, `~/.claude-alt3`). Per i due profili alternativi il match `parent.name == \".claude\"` FALLISCE e il loader non riconosce l'installazione; il fallback di (a) punta comunque al profilo di default anche quando l'utente ne ha configurato un altro, quindi legge la configurazione SBAGLIATA in silenzio invece di fallire. E' esattamente la classe che abbiamo inseguito tutto il giorno -- una proprieta' dedotta da un'ETICHETTA (il nome della directory) invece che da un'identita' RISOLTA (la variabile d'ambiente che la dichiara) -- qui applicata al percorso di installazione. Nota: `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` E' gia' letto altrove (agent_capability.py stesso, install_utils.py, uninstall_nwave.py, rc_smoke), quindi il meccanismo corretto ESISTE e questi siti lo aggirano." proposed_solution="Un unico resolver della home di Claude, che legga `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` e ricada su `~/.claude` SOLO in sua assenza, restituendo un percorso risolto; ogni consumatore lo riceve iniettato e nessuno lo ri-deduce. Eliminare in particolare il riconoscimento per NOME (`\".claude\" in path`, `parent.name == \".claude\"`): la domanda giusta non e' 'questa directory si chiama .claude?' ma 'questa directory E' la config-dir che l'ambiente dichiara?'. Dove il percorso non e' determinabile, degradare LOUD invece di ricadere in silenzio sul default -- leggere la configurazione del profilo sbagliato e' peggio che rifiutare. AT obbligatoria: con `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` impostato a una directory che NON si chiama `.claude`, l'installazione deve essere riconosciuta e i template risolti; e il fallback non deve mai puntare al default quando la variabile e' impostata."
- [ ] installed-hook-invoked-as-module-assumes-dev-repo-on-pythonpath: paradigm=object-oriented defect="UN HOOK INSTALLATO VIENE INVOCATO COME MODULO PYTHON DEL REPO DI SVILUPPO, e sulla macchina di un utente il modulo non esiste. SEGNALATO da un utente (Marcio, 2026-07-24) come errore RICORRENTE: `PreToolUse:Bash hook error -- /usr/bin/python3: Error while finding module specification for 'scripts.hooks.no_verify_reminder' (ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'scripts.hooks')`. L'hook e' quindi configurato come `python3 -m scripts.hooks.no_verify_reminder`, una forma che richiede che `scripts/hooks/` sia importabile -- cioe' che il CWD sia la radice del repo di sviluppo, o che il PYTHONPATH lo contenga. Su una macchina utente, dove gli script hook sono COPIATI altrove al momento dell'installazione (`scripts/install/plugins/des_plugin.py` li distribuisce sotto la config-dir), quel modulo non e' importabile e l'hook fallisce a ogni invocazione. E' una violazione del mandato di portabilita' gia' dichiarato in CLAUDE.md ('DES hook commands: usa la variabile $HOME, mai percorsi hardcoded o .venv') estesa alla forma di invocazione: `-m <modulo>` e' un percorso hardcoded travestito da import. AGGRAVANTE: l'errore e' non-bloccante, quindi l'hook fallisce SILENZIOSAMENTE a ogni comando -- l'utente vede un warning ricorrente e perde del tutto la funzione che l'hook doveva fornire, senza che nulla dichiari che quella protezione non e' attiva. Un hook che non gira e' indistinguibile da un hook che non ha obiezioni." proposed_solution="Invocare l'hook installato per PERCORSO ASSOLUTO risolto rispetto alla config-dir (che va a sua volta risolta dall'ambiente -- vedi la riga `claude-home-assumed-canonical-instead-of-resolved`), non come modulo: `python3 <config-dir>/hooks/no_verify_reminder.py`. Verificare TUTTI gli hook installati, non solo questo: la stessa forma `-m scripts.hooks.X` e' probabilmente replicata, e il difetto e' della CONFIGURAZIONE generata dall'installer, non del singolo hook. Aggiungere una verifica post-installazione che ESEGUA ogni hook configurato una volta e fallisca LOUD se non e' invocabile: oggi l'installazione riporta successo mentre configura hook che non partiranno mai, e il silenzio dell'errore non-bloccante fa il resto."
- [ ] examiner-dispatch-no-write-instruction-collides-with-her-own-session-log-step: paradigm=object-oriented defect="A dispatch that tells the examiner 'do NOT modify any file in the worktree' DIRECTLY CONTRADICTS her own spec's recording step, which appends a row to the charter's Session log. Measured 2026-07-22 on drain-resolves-its-own-actuator: the orchestrator wrote the no-write instruction (to protect uncommitted work), and the examiner resolved the conflict by following her spec and appending anyway. That is the CORRECT precedence -- but only by luck of which instruction she weighted. An examiner who instead obeyed the dispatch would SILENTLY LOSE the session-log record, and the loss is invisible: the ledger record would still exist while the charter -- the only part that travels with the branch, since .nwave/telemetry is gitignored -- would be missing its row. The two instructions are both reasonable in isolation; the defect is that the dispatch template lets them collide at all." proposed_solution="Carve the examiner's OWN ledger and session-log writes out of any no-write instruction, in the dispatch template itself rather than in each orchestrator's prose: the template should emit 'do not modify tracked files EXCEPT your own session-log row on the charter you are examining'. Same class as the --intent-swallowed-by-the-charter-lane defect: a template that permits a self-contradicting envelope. Cheap check that it worked: dispatch an examiner with a no-write instruction and confirm the session-log row still lands."
- [ ] blast-radius-staged-entrypoint-works-where-paths-entrypoint-hangs: paradigm=object-oriented defect="`des blast-radius --repo . --paths <files>` HANGS on high-consumer files -- measured 2026-07-22, killed at 180s on src/des/runtime/interpreter.py, and a sister lane measured the same non-completion at 280s on run_contract_gate.py across two attempts. That non-completion is a standing defect and it has been costing lanes their tier measurement, which then gets GUESSED. But the SAME analysis SUCCEEDS through a different entry point: the `--staged` scope used internally by `des commit-slice`'s tier check completed on the same worktree and returned a full consumer census (refactor.main=331, patch_pyproject.patch_pyproject=23, interpreter.des_spawn=23, interpreter.python_for=17), correctly refusing a declared --tier M as measured L. So the analysis engine is not what hangs; something on the --paths entry path is. WHY THIS MATTERS BEYOND ONE SLICE: the next operator to hit the --paths hang will conclude the TOOL is unusable and guess a tier, when a working measurement route exists two flags away. A guessed tier is not a cautious tier, it is an unmeasured one -- measured here: a deliberately conservative M was still wrong by a full tier." proposed_solution="Diagnose why --paths does not complete where --staged does on the same files (likely candidates: the path-set expansion or a consumer-walk that --staged bounds and --paths does not). Until fixed, make the failure self-explaining per GDP-3/GDP-4: --paths should time-box itself and refuse LOUDLY naming --staged as the working alternative, instead of hanging until an operator kills it. Cheap verification that it worked: run both entry points on src/des/runtime/interpreter.py and confirm either both complete, or --paths refuses within its box naming the alternative."
- [ ] dispatched-agent-cannot-be-woken-by-its-own-background-job: paradigm=object-oriented defect="Un agente dispacciato che lancia un comando in BACKGROUND e poi termina il proprio turno 'in attesa della notifica' resta bloccato per sempre: solo l'ORCHESTRATORE riceve le task-notification dei dispatch Agent/Task, mai un subagente per i propri job di shell in background. SEGNALATO da un utente (Tsunami, 2026-07-24) con caso reale: un agente DISTILL ha lanciato `des verify-red-green` come job backgrounded, ha chiuso il turno dicendo 'aspetto la notifica', e si e' fermato finche' l'orchestratore non se n'e' accorto con un pgrep e non l'ha ripreso a mano. Il costo e' subdolo perche' l'agente NON e' in errore: e' vivo, coerente, e sta aspettando un evento che per costruzione non arrivera'. Dall'esterno e' indistinguibile da un agente lento." proposed_solution="Documentare la convenzione dove vivono le regole di dispatch (la skill che descrive le convenzioni degli agenti dispacciati): un agente dispacciato che ha bisogno del risultato di un comando lungo deve eseguirlo in FOREGROUND dentro il proprio turno, oppure fare polling nello STESSO turno -- mai chiudere il turno 'in attesa di una notifica', che per i propri job in background non arrivera' mai. Il background e' uno strumento dell'ORCHESTRATORE, non del subagente. Vale la pena aggiungerlo anche al prompt di dispatch generato, non solo alla skill: e' esattamente il tipo di regola che si applica a chi non l'ha letta."
- [ ] find-newermt-with-a-relative-time-returns-empty-and-exit-zero: paradigm=object-oriented defect="NOTA DI METODO, non un difetto del prodotto -- filata qui perche' e' costata quattro volte in una sessione e la prossima volta costera' ancora. `find . -newermt \"-5 minutes\"` e' accettato senza errore, restituisce ZERO risultati ed esce 0, anche quando esistono file modificati in quella finestra. MISURATO 2026-07-24: il comando ha riportato 'nessun file toccato' mentre `.nwave/telemetry/atdd-pure/oss-trunk-consolidation.jsonl` era stato scritto 30 secondi prima; `find . -mmin -10` sugli stessi file li elenca correttamente. La forma relativa non viene rifiutata, viene interpretata in un modo che non matcha nulla -- quindi il vuoto si legge come un'assenza verificata quando e' un errore di sintassi silenzioso. STESSA FAMIGLIA gia' pagata oggi altre tre volte: `find ... | head -1 || echo ASSENTE` (il `||` non scatta mai perche' find esce 0 con output vuoto, e sei campi vuoti furono letti come sei assenze), e `cmd | tail; echo $?` (che cattura l'exit di `tail`, non di `cmd`). Il tratto comune, ed e' il vero contenuto di questa riga: **un comando di ricerca che fallisce silenziosamente e' indistinguibile da un comando che ha cercato e non ha trovato**, e nessuna delle due forme lo dichiara." proposed_solution="Per una finestra temporale usare `-mmin -N` / `-mtime`, mai `-newermt` con una stringa relativa; se serve `-newermt`, passargli un timestamp ASSOLUTO. Regola generale che copre tutta la famiglia: prima di leggere un risultato vuoto come un'assenza, VALIDARE il comando su un caso che DEVE dare almeno un risultato -- se la sonda positiva e' vuota, non hai misurato un'assenza, hai misurato un comando rotto. E mai mettere `|| fallback` dopo un comando che esce 0 quando non trova niente: il fallback non scattera' mai e la sua presenza dara' l'impressione che il caso sia gestito."
- [ ] wave-floor-armed-on-an-inference-blocks-instead-of-advising: paradigm=object-oriented defect="Il floor di wave viene armato su una PROVENIENZA INFERITA e da quel momento BLOCCA i dispatch, invece di limitarsi ad avvisare. Violazione diretta di una regola standing di questo repo: un gate decide su un FATTO DICHIARATO, mai su un segnale INFERITO; cio' che e' inferito puo' essere al massimo advisory. MISURATO 2026-07-24: `.nwave/wave-active/active.json` nel worktree in uso conteneva letteralmente `{\"wave\": \"distill\", \"provenance\": \"inferred\", \"armed_at\": <oggi>}` -- il campo dichiara la propria natura congetturale, e cio' nonostante il guard `WAVE_MARKER_BYPASS` ha RIFIUTATO (exit 1, dispatch negato) un finder read-only marcato `DES-MODE: find`, che per la dottrina della spine e' esente dai controlli di completezza del dispatch TDD. Nessuno aveva dichiarato di essere in una wave distill: il sistema l'ha dedotto -- verosimilmente da attivita' DISTILL concorrenti in worktree DIVERSI -- e ha alzato un pavimento nel worktree sbagliato. SECONDO DIFETTO, che moltiplica il primo: il messaggio di recovery propone come terza via `des wave-clear` 'se il floor e' STALE (una wave vecchia di giorni in cui non sei davvero)', ma NON riporta ne' quando il floor e' stato armato, ne' per quale feature, ne' che la provenienza e' inferita. L'operatore bloccato non ha modo di distinguere un floor stantio da uno fresco senza andare a leggere il JSON a mano -- e siccome l'opzione che sblocca e' anche l'unica che DISARMA un controllo, il messaggio spinge verso la scelta piu' pericolosa proprio quando l'informazione per valutarla non c'e'. Nel caso misurato il floor aveva UN'ORA: seguire il consiglio alla lettera avrebbe disarmato un controllo fresco credendolo un residuo." proposed_solution="Un floor con `provenance: inferred` non deve MAI bloccare: deve emettere un avviso e lasciar passare, oppure -- se il blocco e' davvero voluto -- va prima promosso a fatto dichiarato da chi entra nella wave (il dispatch della wave DICHIARA il floor; nessuno lo deduce). E' la stessa asimmetria gia' marchiata altrove: l'inferenza informa, la dichiarazione autorizza. Secondo: il floor va tenuto per-worktree in modo che l'attivita' di un worktree non alzi un pavimento in un altro -- verificare se l'armamento passa da un percorso condiviso, perche' un floor che appare in un albero dove nessuno e' entrato in wave e' un errore di scope, non di logica. Terzo, sul messaggio: ogni rifiuto di wave-floor deve riportare QUANDO il floor e' stato armato, per quale feature e con quale provenienza -- cosi' 'e' stale' diventa una verifica invece che una speranza. E `des wave-clear` deve rifiutarsi o almeno avvertire in modo evidente quando il floor ha meno di N ore, perche' l'operatore che vuole sbloccarsi e' esattamente quello meno adatto a giudicare se il controllo che lo blocca sia legittimo."
